# House bots — the skipped-run register (ruling 275)

> **Authority.** `C5-D20-REPLAN.md` ruling 275 (Ali, 2026-09-17): the **initial** check of a change runs exactly as it
> always has; **repetition** is dropped. Every run a checkpoint skips is written here before that checkpoint closes, with
> the exact command, the store, what the run would prove, and why the skip is safe.
>

> As first written ("until C5-8 has run this file to empty") the rule was UNSATISFIABLE: §1b's row 16 asks for the
> restricted panel on a route that has no page until C7 step 4. A blocking rule that cannot be satisfied is not a
> gate — it is a thing sessions learn to step over, which is how every other gate in this file loses its force.
>

> and ⛔ **row 78 is an EIGHTH**, found 2026-09-21 after the first seven were corrected: it does not name the variable, so a

> section (§1j) rather than a Commit-7 one. It asserts `harness.mjs:29` still defaults to `https://50pick.tz` and that the
> fix "has **not** reached this branch" — both false since `d610a156`. ⭐ **The lesson is the register's own: a stale reason
> spreads by being COPIED between rows, so correcting the rows a ruling names is not the same as correcting the rows it
> unblocks.** Anyone narrowing a rule here should grep the file for the claim, not for the variable.
> and a standing order forbade a session to set it at all. ⭐ **That order was written against a different file.** When it was
> written, `scripts/live/harness.mjs` defaulted to `https://50pick.tz`; **fourteen** scripts import that export, so a run with
> NO environment set — which is how every `npm run` invokes them — drove a real browser against the **LIVE MONEY PLATFORM** as
> ADMIN with Ali's own console password, and 50pick keeps one live session per account, so it revoked his live session.
> **That** is what the prohibition was for, and it was right.
>
> ⭐ **The merge of `origin/main` FIXED THE DEFAULT.** `scripts/live/harness.mjs:51` now reads
> `export const BASE = process.env.LIVE_BASE ?? "http://localhost:3001";` — `d610a156`, 2026-09-19. So an EXPLICIT loopback
> value cannot reach production, and is strictly safer than the unset-and-hope run the old rule was written against.
>
> ⛔ **THE PROHIBITION KEEPS ITS TEETH, NARROWED TO ITS SUBJECT:**
> · `LIVE_BASE` may be set ONLY to `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`, with an explicit port.
> · ⛔ NEVER a hostname, NEVER a public address, NEVER `50pick.tz`, NEVER an unset-and-hope run.
> · ⛔ **Assert the value before using it.** A typo that resolves off-box is the failure this is guarding. The harness prints
>   its target on every run and shouts when the target is not loopback (`harness.mjs:52-59`) — read that line before trusting
>   the run, and treat a production banner as a stop, not as a warning.
> · ⛔ This narrowing is **not permission to point anything at production**, and it is **not a blanket ban to be re-imposed**:
>   a session about to re-impose the blanket ban should read this rule and `d610a156` first, and narrow rather than forbid.
>
> ⚠️ **IT UNBLOCKS THE VARIABLE, NOT THE ROWS.** All seven still need a served app on a port this lane does not own. Every one
> of them stays **OPEN with its run still owed** — a corrected reason is not a run, and recording one must never read as one.

## How to use this file

- **Adding a row** — the checkpoint that skips the run adds it, in its own section, and commits this file by name.
- **Clearing a row** — C5-8 (or the later test session) runs the command, records the printed result in the Result
  column, and moves the row to §3. A row whose run then fails is a finding, not a clearance.
- **Releasing a row** — only Ali releases a run without executing it, and the release is quoted in the row.
- **The Commit-7 sections were triaged** in `C7-DEBT-TRIAGE.md` (2026-09-21), removed from the tree 2026-09-26 as
  spent: a PHASE RECORD, never a register. Every "Full account: `C7-DEBT-TRIAGE.md` …" below reads it with
  `git show 360935a3:plans/house-bots/C7-DEBT-TRIAGE.md`. The four rows it found cannot clear as written
  (**35, 40, 50, 53**) each say so in their own cell. A row still clears here and nowhere else, and
  `npm run -s test:decomment` 2.1 re-derives the carrier count the triage listed by name.

## 2. Owed once for the whole commit (C5-8's own gates, not skips)

These were never per-checkpoint runs; they are listed so nothing is lost between here and C5-8.

| # | Command | Store | What it proves |
|---|---|---|---|
_Empty since 2026-09-20 (C5-8 phase 4): row E — the last row this section held — RAN and is CLEARED in §3. **§2e below is NOT retired with it**: it is the decision that fixed what the batch's scope means, and the clearance in §3 is refused unless it quotes both of §2e's numbers._

### 2e. THE DECISION — what "the ONE mutation batch" means for Commit 5 (C5-8 phase 1, 2026-09-20)

⛔ **THIS DECISION EXISTS BECAUSE ROW E COULD HAVE BEEN CLOSED DISHONESTLY.** Row E says "the ONE mutation batch"
without naming a population. `npm run red:all -- --filter house-bot` is the only batch command that exists, and it
is a **different population** from the four Commit-5 registers row 5 names. Running it and reporting "the batch ran"
would satisfy row E in words while measuring **9 of 262** Commit-5 entries — the "true measurement, wrong
population" failure this programme has already paid for. The scope is therefore fixed here, in the register, before
anything runs.

**WHAT WAS MEASURED (re-derived at this HEAD, never quoted from a row).** Every number below was parsed from the
files themselves; the scripts are throwaway and the method is stated so it can be refuted.

· **There is no runner, and there never was one.** `npm run` has no key matching `mutat|batch|c5-|tools/`. No file
in the repository reads a `*-mutations.json` — the only two that named the pattern were the workflow **authoring**
scripts `tools/c5-step-fast.js:118` and `tools/c7-step.js:127` (both removed from the tree 2026-09-26 as spent), and both instructed an agent to **write** a register,
never to read one. `tools/mutate-c5-s12.mjs` (removed from the tree 2026-09-26 as spent) was marked `⛔ RECORD ONLY` on its own first line, hard-coded its list,
and pointed at `C:/kipindi-hb-mut5a` and a scratchpad that no longer exist. **The registers are write-only by
construction.** Every brief that speaks of "running the register" describes a program that was never built.

· **The four Commit-5 registers hold 262 id-bearing entries in THREE INCOMPATIBLE GRAMMARS.** `c5-s4` 85 (string
longhand, `ID · file · ` + backticked `from → to` + ` · suite · assertions`); `c5-s5` 140 (the only machine-shaped
grammar, objects with literal `from`/`to`); `c5-5b` 26 = 1 header + 25 (string longhand, a *different* shape); `c5-s7`
14 = 2 prose + 12 (string longhand that self-labels `DECLARED as <anchor name>`). A single runner would need three
parsers, and for 18 of the entries no parser is possible at all.

· **Exactly 9 of the 262 are reachable by an existing harness** — `c5-s7`'s S7-M01…M09, declared in
`scripts/anchors/house-bot-console.anchors.mjs` under the `reports-mem` suite key. Measured by exact `from`-text
identity against the four house-bot anchors modules. ⚠️ **The zero for the other three registers was refuted before
it was believed:** a whole-corpus substring test flagged 11 `c5-s4` from-texts as "appearing" in some anchors file,
and every one of the 11 failed the exact-declaration test — they occur as `to` texts or inside prose, not as
declarations. Appearing in a file is not reachability.

· **What `red:all --filter house-bot` actually runs is 366 declarations, not 327 and not 331.** The filter selects
exactly three keys (`red:house-bot-console` 273, `red:house-bot-money` 47 + seam 7, `red:house-bot-engine` 39).
The 327 in the older brief was correct when written and has since moved with the branch; **re-derive it again before
the run** rather than quoting this line. Of the 366, **nine** are Commit-5 register entries and **357** belong to
Commits 6 and 7.

**THE DISPOSITION OF ALL 262, BY CATEGORY.** The five categories are exhaustive and disjoint: 18 + 49 + 90 + 4 + 101
= 262.

· **49 are MOOT — their subject was deleted by Commit 5's own D20 un-build.** They target four files that commit
`7c754515` removed: `src/lib/house-bot/exposure-copy.ts` (28), `src/lib/server/house-bot/exposure.ts` (13),
`src/components/admin/exposure-line.tsx` (6) and `src/app/admin/kyc/[id]/bets-placed.tsx` (2). ⛔ These are **REMOVED,
not rotted and not skipped**: a mutation of code a ruling deliberately struck proves nothing, and counting one as
"run" would be the worst reading of this register. They are never to be re-anchored.

· **18 have a PROSE from-text and are not machine-applicable by any runner** (1 in `c5-s4`, 11 in `c5-5b`, 6 in
`c5-s7`): they describe their anchor rather than quoting it — "the three-line window … at :2792", "that same line
PLUS its trailing newline". They are **longhand or they are nothing**, and this decision says longhand: each is run
by hand, or it is recorded NOT MEASURED naming this line. No runner will ever reach them.

· **90 have ROTTED to zero** against the working tree — their `from` no longer occurs, because the code moved under
them since 2026-09-17. They cannot be run by anything until they are re-anchored, and re-anchoring 90 entries is a
piece of work in its own right, not a checkpoint afterthought.

· **4 are AMBIGUOUS** (`from` matches more than once). `red-anchor.mjs` refuses these by design rather than
injecting into the wrong site, so they are in the same position as the rotted 90.

· **101 resolve exactly once today.** Nine of those are the already-declared `c5-s7` set, so **92 entries are the
genuinely runnable, genuinely owed remainder.**

**THE DECISION, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT COVER.**

1. **"The ONE mutation batch" of row E means the DECLARED population: `npm run red:all -- --filter house-bot`,
   re-derived at run time (366 at this HEAD), run sequentially from a tree that matches `HEAD` exactly, with an
   explicit large `--timeout`.** That is the batch. It is a real and large proof and it should be reported as
   exactly what it is.
2. **It is reported as covering 9 of the 262 Commit-5 register entries — never as covering the registers.** Row E's
   clearance must quote both numbers. A clearance that says only "the batch ran" is refused by this block.
3. **A RUNNER IS NOT TO BE BUILT FOR THE REGISTERS AS THEY STAND, and this is the sizing.** Of 262 entries, 67 can
   never be run by a runner (49 moot + 18 prose) and 94 cannot be run until they are re-anchored (90 rotted + 4
   ambiguous). A general three-grammar runner would therefore be built to serve 101 entries, 9 of which already run
   every day. ⭐ **The sanctioned route for the remaining 92 is CONVERSION, not a runner:** written as declarations
   under `scripts/anchors/`, they join `test:red-anchors` §3, which resolves every `from` against the tree **every
   day** and would have reported all 90 rotted entries as they rotted. That is the instrument this programme already
   trusts, and it is the one that stops the rot recurring. A bespoke runner reading JSON that nothing else validates
   would rebuild the same decay.
4. **THE 92 ARE OWED AND ARE NOT CLEARED BY THIS CHECKPOINT.** They are carried as row 93 below. ⛔ Commit 5 does not
   close on the claim that its registers were run. It closes, if it closes, on the declared batch plus an explicit,
   written statement that 253 register entries were dispositioned rather than measured — 67 of them permanently, 94
   pending a re-anchor, and 92 pending conversion.
   🟡 **CARRIED OUT IN PART 2026-09-21, and the sizing in point 3 above did not survive contact — read it as this
   block's reasoning, not as this tree's numbers.** `scripts/anchors/house-bot-c5.anchors.mjs` declares **69**
   entries (64 straight + 5 re-anchored), retiring **75** of the 262 once the 3 that duplicate a `c5-5b` site are
   counted. Re-derived against the working tree with the same resolver `test:red-anchors` §3 injects with, the
   disposition is **75 retired + 9 already declared + 76 moot + 102 still owed**, not the 49/18/90/4/101 this block
   records: the earlier split was produced by a parser that could not read every grammar's `from` cell (it left 18
   entries as "prose" that are ordinary anchors, and reported 4 as ambiguous that are not), and the MOOT population
   is 76 rather than 49 because 27 entries keep a file that survives while the SUBJECT inside it was struck. ⛔ The
   tree did not change under these two counts — **the instrument did**, which is the argument for declaring in the
   first place. ⛔ And the conversion does NOT clear row 93: declaring is not driving, and the drive is row 96.
5. **Ali's release is NOT assumed for any of it.** No row here is closed by inferring consent; where a release is the
   right answer it must be quoted in the row in his own words.

⚠️ **THE HONEST SUMMARY, for whoever writes the close:** the batch Commit 5 can actually run proves the *declared*
house-bot anchors can fail. It does **not** prove that the assertions Commit 5's own four registers describe can
fail. Saying so plainly is the smaller claim, and it is the true one.

It cannot be: **`buildDsarBundle` builds BOTH documents**, so the trigger player's and the twin's each gain the same key, and §7 compares them to each other — two documents that both gained a key have the same key set and the same normalised shape. Only a DIFFERENTIAL mutation can redden an equality. Measured: 40 passed / 3 failed, the §7 lines GREEN, and what went red instead was §6's officer-bundle door on the vocabulary belt ("liquidity"). ⭐ The register entry is corrected in the same commit, and `X86-C` in row 86 is `S7-X02` rewritten so it can fail on the claim it names. **What did not wait for any of this:** every absence assertion C5-7 added carries its own planted control inside the suite, run on every run. | 2026-09-20 |
